SINCE I'M BUILDING A STAR TRADER DISCOVERY AND SELECTION PLATFIORM TO DISCOVER THE BEST TRADERS WHO MAY NOT YET KNOW THEY'VE GOT THE INSTINCT FOR TRADING, I WANT TO DRAFT A CONTRACT THAT ENABLES US TO BE ABLE TO HAVE THE RIFGHT OF FIRST REFUSAL ON HIRING AND CAPITAL ALLOCATION, CAPITAL MANAGEMENT LOCKOUT PERIOD OF SOME YEARS WHERE THEY CAN'T START A FUND, OR FUNDRAISE, OR TRADE FOR OR MANAGE CAPITAL AT A  FIRM NOT ENDORSED BY US( TRADEQUIP ), AND IF EMPLOYED, CANNOT COMPETE FOR EMPLOYMENT OR JUMP SHI[P WITHIN TYHE TRADING AND FUND MANAGEMENT INDUSTRY FOR A YEAR. USE THESE RESOURCES AND THE WIDER INTERNET FOR RESEARCH ::   GOGLOBAL.COM    JUSTWORKS.COM, LEGALTREE.NL, SOLV, LEGAL.COM   MODULAR DRAFTING STRATEGY, 6-12 MONTHS FOR NON COMPETE, PERPETUAL RIGHT OF FIRST REFUSAL/ACCEPTANCE, DEEL.COM  INDIA SPECIFIC, EAST AFRICA, WEST AFRICA, MIDDLE EAST GENERAL, UAE SPECIFIC, KENYA SPECIFIC, NIGERIA SPECIFIC ,  S.E ASIA REGION, S.AMERICA, EUROPE, CHECK  CLAUSES, ENFORCEABILITY, AND ADAPTATIONS FOR EACH REGION/COUNTRY SPECIFIC AND NUANCES IN LEGAL SETTLEMENTS
Thought for 2m 43s

Below is a modular contract package designed for a “star trader discovery → first capital → long-term retention” pipeline. It is engineered so you can dial restrictions up/down by jurisdiction while still protecting Tradequip’s core interests: (i) proprietary methods, (ii) investor relationships, (iii) platform-sourced track record, (iv) introductions, and (v) continuity of capital deployment.

This is not legal advice. You will still want local counsel to validate employee vs contractor classification, statutory limits, and drafting conventions in each target country—especially because enforceability of non-competes varies widely and is evolving in several markets.

1) Reality check: what you can (and cannot) reliably enforce globally
Generally enforceable almost everywhere (if drafted narrowly)

Confidentiality / trade secrets (often survives indefinitely for true trade secrets).

IP assignment (for platform code, signals, research, model specs).

Non-solicitation / non-circumvention (clients/investors you introduce, and your traders/employees).

Track-record use restrictions (e.g., “you may not market platform-generated performance as yours without our consent”).

Exclusivity during the relationship (while they are in your program / managing your capital).

The “high variance” items (must be modular by country)

Post-termination non-compete:

India: post-termination non-competes in employment are generally void under Section 27; recent case commentary continues to reaffirm this.

Strategic implication for Tradequip

To get the outcome you want (“multi-year lockout”), the most enforceable design is:

Multi-year exclusivity during engagement (while they manage Tradequip capital, or while they are in a funded program), plus

Short post-termination restrictions (6–12 months) where legal, and

Long-tail controls that are not a non-compete (ROFR/ROFO, non-circumvention, track-record marketing limits, non-solicit, confidentiality).

2) The recommended modular document set (do this instead of one giant agreement)
Document A — Trader Discovery Participation + Data/Track-Record + ROFR (Pre-hire)

Applies to anyone using your selection platform (even before employment). This is where you lock:

ownership/usage of platform performance data,

confidentiality,

non-circumvention of introductions,

Right of First Refusal / Right of First Offer for capital allocation and hiring (structured as a process right, not a blanket restraint).

Document B — Funded Trader Engagement Agreement (Employment or Contractor)

Used once they become “funded” or hired. This is where you add:

exclusivity while engaged,

post-termination non-compete only where permitted, with mandatory compensation language when required (EU),

garden leave / notice period,

remedies (injunction, clawback, liquidated damages where allowed).

Document C — Capital Allocation / Managed Account Terms

If you allocate capital but do not “employ,” you can still impose:

“no outside AUM management during term,”

conflicts of interest controls,

strategy/IP protection,

key-man / suspension rights,

long notice period (commercially negotiated).

3) Draft Template (Modular): TRADEQUIP TRADER DISCOVERY & CAPITAL RIGHTS AGREEMENT

Use this as Document A (pre-hire). Then attach the relevant country addendum.

TRADEQUIP TRADER DISCOVERY & CAPITAL RIGHTS AGREEMENT
Effective Date: [DATE]
Parties: (1) Tradequip [Legal Entity Name, Country, Registration #] (“Tradequip”) and (2) [Trader Full Legal Name] (“Trader”)
Program/Platform: Tradequip Trader Discovery & Selection Platform (“Platform”)

0. Recitals

A. Trader seeks access to the Platform, evaluation tools, and potential funding/hiring pathways.
B. Tradequip will provide access and may provide training, analytics, coaching, and/or introductions.
C. The Parties agree to protect Tradequip’s confidential information, investor relationships, and platform-generated performance data.

1. Definitions (key ones)

“Performance Data” = all trading records, analytics, dashboards, rankings, risk metrics, journaling, and evaluation outputs generated by or through the Platform (including derived metrics).

“Tradequip Introduction” = any introduction to an investor, capital provider, allocator, institution, fund, proprietary desk, or recruiting partner made by Tradequip.

“External Opportunity” = any offer or opportunity for Trader to (i) manage third-party capital, (ii) be hired/engaged as a trader/PM, (iii) start/operate a fund, (iv) raise capital, or (v) provide trading services for compensation.

“Restricted Business” = [define narrowly: e.g., “discretionary or systematic trading of FX/CFDs/indices/commodities/equities using strategies substantially similar to Tradequip Methods”].

“Tradequip Methods” = Tradequip’s proprietary models, evaluation criteria, risk guardrails, signals, playbooks, selection heuristics, platform logic, and research.

2. Consideration

In exchange for Trader’s commitments, Tradequip provides: Platform access; evaluation; analytics; potential coaching; and eligibility for funding/hiring consideration.

3. Confidentiality (global core)

Trader shall not disclose or use Tradequip Confidential Information except for Platform participation.
Term: during participation and thereafter indefinitely for trade secrets; and for other confidential info for [3–5] years (or maximum allowed).

4. IP + Work Product

All platform-related work product created specifically for Tradequip (research notes you submit into the Platform, strategy writeups requested by Tradequip, risk templates, code, indicators) is assigned to Tradequip to the extent permitted by law.
Trader retains general trading skill/experience not constituting Tradequip Methods or Confidential Information.

5. Performance Data ownership + marketing restrictions

5.1 Ownership: Performance Data is owned by Tradequip (or licensed to Tradequip with an irrevocable, worldwide license) to operate the Platform and evaluate Trader.
5.2 Trader license: Trader receives a limited license to view personal stats for personal use.
5.3 No track-record marketing without consent: Trader may not publish, sell, market, or present Platform Performance Data as an audited track record to raise capital or gain employment without Tradequip’s prior written consent, except where required by law.

6. Right of First Refusal / First Offer (capital + hiring)

This clause is drafted to be process-based, not an outright ban.

6.1 Notice obligation: Trader must promptly notify Tradequip of any External Opportunity (written notice with material terms).
6.2 Right of First Refusal (ROFR): For any External Opportunity involving managing capital or trading employment/engagement, Tradequip has the right to match the bona fide offer on substantially similar terms within [15–30] days. If Tradequip matches, Trader must accept Tradequip’s matched offer.
6.3 Right of First Offer (ROFO) fallback: If Trader intends to pursue capital raising or a funded seat without a third-party offer, Trader must first offer Tradequip an exclusive negotiation window of [30–45] days.
6.4 Re-offer if improved terms: If Trader accepts an External Opportunity on terms materially better than the notice terms (e.g., compensation/AUM/fee share improves by >5–10%), Trader must re-offer to Tradequip.
6.5 Duration (“Perpetual” design without a perpetual restraint):

(a) This ROFR/ROFO applies during Platform participation and for [5–10] years thereafter; and

(b) Indefinitely for opportunities arising directly from a Tradequip Introduction or that rely materially on non-public Performance Data or Tradequip Confidential Information.
(Indefinite blanket restraints are commonly attacked as unreasonable; the “indefinite” tail here is tied only to introductions/data rights, not to Trader’s general ability to work.)

7. Non-circumvention (introductions) — recommended everywhere

Trader shall not circumvent Tradequip by directly or indirectly soliciting, onboarding, contracting with, or receiving capital from any Tradequip Introduction without Tradequip’s written consent for [24 months] after the last introduction contact.

8. Exclusivity during participation (optional but powerful)

During participation (and any funded trial), Trader will not:

manage outside third-party capital,

raise capital, or

trade for another firm/desk
without Tradequip’s written consent.
(“During term” exclusivity is typically far easier to enforce than post-term non-competes.)

9. Post-termination restrictive covenants (MODULE — only where permitted)

This entire Section 9 is jurisdiction-dependent. If used, it must be narrow and compensable where required.

9.1 Non-solicitation of investors/clients: for [12–24 months], Trader will not solicit Tradequip investors, allocators, clients, or capital partners with whom Trader had contact through Tradequip.
9.2 Non-solicitation of workforce: for [12–24 months], Trader will not solicit Tradequip employees/traders.
9.3 Non-compete (6–12 months) (OPTIONAL): for [6–12 months], Trader will not engage in the Restricted Business within [Territory] in a role substantially similar to the role performed with Tradequip.
9.4 Compensation (EU module): If required by local law or best practice, Tradequip will pay post-term restriction compensation per Schedule C (e.g., NL proposed 50% when invoked; Germany 50% statutory; France often 25–50%). 
L&E Global
+2
Mayer Brown
+2

10. Lock-out against “starting a fund / fundraising” (how to do this without overreaching)

Instead of “you can’t start a fund for X years” (high litigation risk), use this structure:

10.1 No fundraising using Tradequip Data: Trader may not raise capital using Platform Performance Data or any Tradequip Confidential Information without consent (ties back to Section 5).
10.2 Non-circumvention of Tradequip investors: Trader may not solicit or accept capital from Tradequip investors/introductions for [24–36 months] (Schedule B list/definition).
10.3 If you want a multi-year lock, do it as a funded-term commitment: Put the multi-year “lock” into Document B/C as a minimum funded term (e.g., 3 years) with an early exit buyout, not as a post-term non-compete.

11. Remedies

11.1 Injunctive relief (where available), plus damages.
11.2 Liquidated damages (optional): pre-estimated damages for breach of non-circumvention / track-record misuse (must be reasonable; some jurisdictions treat penalties as unenforceable).
11.3 Fee-shifting (if permitted).

12. Dispute Resolution; Governing Law

Use a Country Addendum to set governing law/venue/arbitration appropriate for the Trader’s work location.

Employment-related disputes may be subject to mandatory local forums regardless of contract wording.

13. Severability / Blue-pencil / Reformation

If any restraint is unenforceable, it should be reduced to the maximum enforceable scope and the remainder survives.

Schedules

Schedule A: Trader profile + markets + role scope

Schedule B: Restricted Business definition + territory

Schedule C: Post-term compensation (if required)

Schedule D: Country Addendum (pick one below)

4) Country/Region Addenda (what to change)
A) INDIA (India-specific addendum — do NOT use post-termination non-compete)

Positioning: Indian employment law generally treats post-termination non-competes as void under Section 27; recent reporting continues to reaffirm that even “reasonable” post-employment restraints are not recognized in employment contracts.

Add training repayment / sign-on clawback (careful drafting; must be proportional).

ROFR/ROFO can remain, but ensure it is a process right and not framed as “you cannot work elsewhere.”

B) KENYA (Kenya-specific addendum)

Kenya generally follows a reasonableness approach (legitimate interest + reasonable scope/time/territory). Keep non-compete short (6–12 months) and define scope tightly. 
tradesecretslaw.com

Kenya drafting tweaks:

Keep non-compete 6 months as default; expand to 12 only for senior PMs with material confidential access.

Territory: Kenya or East Africa only if you can justify it commercially.

Prioritize non-solicit + non-circumvention and paid garden leave where possible.

C) NIGERIA (Nigeria-specific addendum)

Nigeria applies common law restraint-of-trade principles; enforceability depends heavily on reasonableness (duration/geography/scope) and legitimate interest. 
IBA
+1

Nigeria drafting tweaks:

Non-compete: 6–12 months; narrow to specific role/strategy scope.

Emphasize non-solicit, confidentiality, and non-circumvention.

Remedies: injunction may be sought, but reasonableness will be scrutinized.

D) UAE (UAE-specific addendum)

UAE non-competes are commonly cited as capped at ≤2 years and must specify time/place/type of work; official guidance notes the 2-year maximum.

Across parts of the region, non-competes are commonly enforceable if reasonable, with durations often up to 1–2 years depending on country; Saudi guidance commonly references up to 2 years if properly limited.

F) SE Asia (region) — Singapore example (reasonableness + legitimate interest)

Singapore treats non-competes as prima facie void unless protecting legitimate proprietary interest and reasonable; courts scrutinize duration/geo/scope, and “indefinite” restraints are likely problematic.

SE Asia drafting approach:

Keep non-compete to 3–6 months for most roles; extend only for senior strategy roles with documented confidential exposure.

Never draft “perpetual non-compete.” Use perpetual confidentiality for trade secrets instead.

G) South America (region) — Brazil example (compensation norms)

Brazil commonly expects indemnification/compensation during the restricted period; one-month salary per month of restriction is a frequently cited benchmark in practice commentary.

If you don’t want compensation complexity, lean on: confidentiality + non-solicit + non-circumvention + track-record use restrictions.

5) How to achieve your “multi-year lockout” goal without drafting an unenforceable restraint

If your business objective is:

“They can’t start a fund, fundraise, or manage capital at a non-endorsed firm for years.”

The enforceable architecture is:

Multi-year funded term (during engagement)

“Trader agrees to a 3-year funded term while managing Tradequip capital.”

“No outside capital management or fundraising during term.”

Early exit → buyout (objective formula) + immediate cessation of access + IP return.

Post-term (6–12 months) only where legal

Use non-compete only where permitted (and compensable where required).

Otherwise use garden leave/notice as the “cooling off.”

Long-tail protection that isn’t a non-compete

Non-circumvention of Tradequip Introductions (24–36 months).

No marketing of platform Performance Data to raise capital without consent (ongoing).

Confidentiality/trade secrets (ongoing).

This approach is far more defensible than “years of post-termination no-fundraising.”

6) Implementation checklist (what your counsel should confirm per country)

Worker classification: employee vs contractor (misclassification risk can destroy enforcement).

Whether post-term non-compete is allowed; if yes:

max duration,

required compensation,

formalities (written form, justification, territory specificity),

whether enforcement differs for fixed-term vs indefinite contracts.

Whether liquidated damages are treated as penalties.

Whether arbitration clauses are permitted/typical for employment disputes.

Data privacy rules for storing and using Performance Data cross-border.

THE FUNDING MODEL WILL BE DIFFERENT; THE MODEL WILL INVOLVE THE VIRTUAL WINNERS SELECTED TO WORK FOR A FUND MANAGEMENT COMPANY WHICH MANAGES FUNDS.
SEE BELOW AND KEEP CONTEXT IN REDOING OUTPUT:
-Hedge fund THE MAIN CAPITAL FUND
-Management company (WHERE TRADERS WILL BE EMPLOYED UPON SELECTION)
-Trader growth + vetting platform (product sub-brand FOR WHERE THE TRADER FUNNELING FROM SIGNUP TO TRADE TO TIERED PERFORMANCE, TO SELECTION OCCURS)
Thought for 1m 41s

Under your three-entity model, the most defensible way to get the commercial outcome you want is to split obligations across (i) the Trader Growth + Vetting Platform stage (pre-hire), and (ii) the Management Company employment stage (post-selection), while treating the Hedge Fund as the capital vehicle that the Management Company manages.

This matters because many jurisdictions will not enforce broad “you can’t work / can’t fundraise” restraints after termination (e.g., India), so you need a structure that relies heavily on (a) term-based exclusivity, (b) non-circumvention / non-solicit, (c) track-record/data-use controls, and (d) ROFR/ROFO rights tied to Tradequip-introduced opportunities and Tradequip-generated performance data, rather than a blanket multi-year post-termination ban. (This aligns with how global restrictive covenant guidance frames enforceability differences and the need for alternatives.)

Below is a redo of the drafting approach and a ready modular contract package, rewritten to match:

Hedge Fund = the main capital fund (the client/vehicle)

Management Company = the fund manager / advisor entity that employs selected traders

Trader Growth + Vetting Platform = the product/sub-brand and funnel

1) Contract stack that matches your structure
Document 1 (Pre-hire, everyone signs):

Tradequip Platform Participation + Opportunity Rights Agreement
Signed by Trader + Platform Entity (and optionally countersigned by the Management Company as a third-party beneficiary).

Purpose: lock in (a) confidentiality/IP, (b) performance-data ownership and marketing limits, (c) non-circumvention of introductions, and (d) ROFR/ROFO on employment with the Management Company and capital allocation/PM seat.

Document 2 (Post-selection, only winners):

Management Company Employment Agreement + Restrictive Covenant Addendum
Signed by Trader + Management Company.

Purpose: enforce term exclusivity and (where legal) a 6–12 month post-termination non-compete + non-solicit + garden leave/notice mechanics. Note: EU/Brazil often require compensation for post-term non-competes (Netherlands proposals cite 50% during invocation; Germany requires at least 50%; France commonly 25–50% with “non-negligible” minimums). 
Loyens & Loeff
+4
LawNow
+4
Heuking
+4

Document 3 (Internal—employment attachment or policy):

PM Mandate / Trading Authority Policy (Fund-facing)
Issued by Management Company for the Hedge Fund program (not necessarily signed as a contract in every jurisdiction, but acknowledged by the employee).

Purpose: defines what the trader may do with Fund capital, compliance rules, risk limits, personal trading policy, conflicts, recordkeeping, audit trails, and termination/suspension triggers.

2) The “Master” definitions you should use everywhere

Define the group cleanly so rights flow correctly:

“Tradequip Platform” = the trader growth + vetting platform brand/product.

“Platform Operator” = the entity that owns/operates the Platform (could be a software company).

“Tradequip Management Company” = the regulated/operating manager that employs traders and provides investment management services.

“Tradequip Fund(s)” = the hedge fund vehicle(s) managed by the Management Company (may be third-party beneficiaries).

Use “Tradequip Group” to refer collectively to all three, and state that certain provisions are for the benefit of each as applicable (confidentiality, non-circumvention, track-record/data restrictions, ROFR/ROFO).

3) Document 1 Template: Platform Participation + Opportunity Rights Agreement (pre-hire)

TRADEQUIP PLATFORM PARTICIPATION & OPPORTUNITY RIGHTS AGREEMENT
Parties: (1) [Platform Operator legal name] (“Platform Operator”) and (2) [Trader full legal name] (“Trader”).
Third-Party Beneficiaries: Tradequip Management Company and Tradequip Fund(s) (together with Platform Operator, “Tradequip Group”).
Effective Date: [DATE]

3.1 Performance Data + Track Record Control (core protection)

Performance Data ownership/license: All platform-generated trading records, rankings, analytics, and evaluation outputs (“Performance Data”) are owned by Platform Operator, with a perpetual right for Tradequip Group to use them for evaluation, compliance, marketing (with Trader consent where required), and investment decisioning.

No capital-raising marketing using Platform Data: Trader may not use Performance Data (or derivative excerpts like Sharpe, win-rate, equity curve screenshots) to solicit capital, obtain a PM seat, or market a “track record” without Tradequip Group’s written approval, except where required by law.

This is one of the most enforceable “long-tail” controls across jurisdictions because it regulates data and representations, not the right to work.

3.2 Introductions + Non-Circumvention (core protection)

Define “Tradequip Introduction” = any allocator, investor, fund, prop desk, recruiter, or capital provider introduced by Tradequip Group.

Trader must not solicit, contract with, accept capital from, or otherwise circumvent any Tradequip Introduction for 24–36 months after last contact, unless Tradequip consents in writing.

3.3 ROFR/ROFO (your “right of first refusal”—reframed to fit the model)

This is where you anchor your “first shot” at employment and allocation:

A) ROFR on Employment / Engagement (Management Company first)

If Trader receives any offer to trade or manage capital for a third party (employment, contractor, PM seat, advisory), Trader must provide written notice with material terms.

Management Company ROFR: Tradequip Management Company has [15–30] days to match the offer on substantially similar terms. If matched, Trader must accept the Management Company’s offer.

B) ROFO on “Starting a Fund / Fundraising” (process right)
Because “you can’t start a fund for years” is often attacked as an overbroad restraint, do it as a first-offer process:

If Trader intends to (i) launch a fund/vehicle, (ii) raise capital, or (iii) accept third-party capital allocations, Trader must first give Tradequip Management Company an exclusive negotiation window of [30–45] days to:

hire Trader and develop the strategy internally, and/or

sponsor the strategy within a Tradequip Fund sleeve, and/or

act as the manager/advisor for the vehicle.

C) “Perpetual” component (make it legally survivable)
Do not make a blanket perpetual restraint. Make the “long tail” perpetual only as to:

opportunities arising from a Tradequip Introduction, and/or

fundraising or allocations that rely on non-public Performance Data or Tradequip Confidential Information.

This is significantly more defensible than “perpetual ROFR for any job anywhere.”

3.4 Confidentiality / Trade Secrets

Confidentiality survives indefinitely for trade secrets; otherwise 3–5 years.

3.5 Assignment / IP

Any platform-specific submissions you create at Tradequip’s request (strategy writeups, tooling, indicators, research notes) are assigned to Tradequip Group to the extent permitted.

3.6 Jurisdiction Addendum (Schedule)

Attach the country module (see Section 5 below). India, in particular, needs special handling because post-termination non-competes are generally not enforceable.

4) Document 2 Template: Management Company Employment Agreement (selected traders)

TRADEQUIP MANAGEMENT COMPANY – TRADER EMPLOYMENT AGREEMENT
Employer: Tradequip Management Company [legal name]
Employee: [Trader]
Assignment: Employee will trade/manage strategies on behalf of Tradequip Fund(s) per PM Mandate Policy.

4.1 Exclusivity during employment (high enforceability globally)

During employment, Trader will not manage outside third-party capital, trade for another firm, or provide trading services without written consent.

4.2 Post-termination restrictions (modular)

Use these “layers,” and turn on/off based on jurisdiction:

Non-solicitation (investors/clients introduced by Tradequip): 12–24 months

Non-solicitation (employees/traders): 12–24 months

Non-compete (6–12 months): only where permitted; narrowly defined role + strategy scope + territory.

Garden leave / notice period: use as a substitute where non-competes are weak or banned.

Compensation where required (EU/Brazil): explicitly include the statutory/market compensation mechanics (Germany requires at least 50% for post-contractual non-competes; Netherlands reform proposals cite 50% while invoked; France commonly requires meaningful compensation—often cited 25–50%, with practical minimums discussed around ~33%). 
Loyens & Loeff
+4
Heuking
+4
LawNow
+4

4.3 “One-year no jump ship” requirement (how to implement safely)

Instead of a hard “you cannot work in the industry for a year” (often too broad), implement:

A 6–12 month non-compete limited to “portfolio manager/trader roles managing third-party capital in [defined products/strategies]” only where legal; and/or

Garden leave + notice (paid) to create a functional cooling-off period; and

Deferred bonus/carry vesting with forfeiture for competitive departure (needs local wage/penalty review).

4.4 US-specific note (if you hire US traders)

The FTC’s 2024 noncompete rule was blocked and the FTC later moved to abandon the appeal; enforcement remains largely state-by-state. 
Federal Trade Commission

American Physical Therapy Association

6) What you should treat as “Group-wide must-haves” (works in almost every jurisdiction)

If you want maximum cross-border survivability, prioritize these as your non-negotiables (and make them apply to all three entities via “Tradequip Group”):

Performance Data ownership + no fundraising using Platform track record without consent

Non-circumvention of Tradequip Introductions (24–36 months)

ROFR/ROFO (employment + allocation) tied to introductions/data, not a blanket restraint

Confidentiality/trade secrets (indefinite for trade secrets)

Non-solicitation (investors + workforce)

Term-based exclusivity during Management Company employment

Garden leave/notice as fallback where non-competes are weak/banned

This approach is consistent with widely cited global guidance that enforceability differs materially by country and that employers often need alternative protective tools when non-competes are constrained.

DOCUMENT 1 — TRADEQUIP PLATFORM PARTICIPATION + OPPORTUNITY RIGHTS + CAREER REPRESENTATION AGREEMENT

(Signed at signup / first platform access)

Effective Date: [DATE]
Platform Operator: [LEGAL NAME OF PLATFORM COMPANY], a [jurisdiction] company (“Platform Operator”)
Trader: [FULL LEGAL NAME], (“Trader”)
Tradequip Group Beneficiaries:

Management Company: [LEGAL NAME OF MANAGEMENT COMPANY], (“Management Company”)

Fund(s): [LEGAL NAME OF HEDGE FUND(S)] (each a “Fund”)
Platform Operator, Management Company, and Fund(s) are collectively “Tradequip Group.”

IMPORTANT NOTICE: This Agreement is not an employment agreement. Participation does not guarantee selection, funding, or employment.

1. Purpose and Recognition of Opportunity

1.1 Platform-designed discovery. Trader acknowledges that Tradequip Group has created the Platform to identify and develop trading talent that many individuals would not otherwise discover or prove in a structured, auditable environment.

1.2 Consideration and exchange. In exchange for Platform access, evaluation, ranking, coaching content (if any), infrastructure, and the possibility of selection for professional trading roles, Trader agrees to the Opportunity Rights and Career Representation obligations set out in this Agreement.

1.3 No guarantee. Tradequip Group may, at its sole discretion, select or not select any Trader, modify selection criteria, pause programs, or limit hiring based on compliance, risk, or business needs.

2. Definitions

Capitalized terms are defined in Schedule 1 (Definitions).

3. Term; Program Stages

3.1 Term. This Agreement begins on the Effective Date and continues until terminated under Section 12.

3.2 Stages. Trader’s participation may include:

Stage A: general signup and onboarding

Stage B: tiered performance evaluation

Stage C: “Selected Candidate” designation (if achieved)

Stage D: offer process for employment with Management Company and/or placement with Partner Management Companies.

4. Platform Rules; Integrity; No Manipulation

4.1 Trader will follow Platform rules, including identity verification steps, trading rules, risk guardrails, and integrity requirements.

4.2 Trader will not manipulate evaluation results or attempt to circumvent Platform controls (including multi-accounting, collusion, or simulated performance falsification). Tradequip Group may suspend access, disqualify performance, or terminate this Agreement.

5. Performance Data; Track Record; Marketing Restrictions

5.1 Performance Data ownership. All Performance Data generated by or through the Platform (including derived metrics, rankings, analytics, and evaluation outputs) is owned by Platform Operator. Platform Operator grants Tradequip Group a perpetual, worldwide right to use Performance Data for evaluation, compliance, program operations, and internal investment decision-making.

5.2 Trader access license. Trader receives a limited, revocable license to view personal Performance Data for personal informational use.

5.3 No external marketing as an “audited track record” without consent. Trader shall not publish, sell, license, market, or present Platform Performance Data (or screenshots/exports/derivatives) as a track record to obtain employment, raise capital, or solicit allocations without prior written consent from Platform Operator (and, where applicable, Management Company), except where required by law.

5.4 Truthfulness. If Trader is authorized to reference Platform performance, Trader must do so accurately and with required disclaimers provided by Tradequip Group.

6. Confidentiality; Trade Secrets

6.1 Confidential Information. Trader shall keep confidential and not misuse Tradequip Group Confidential Information, including evaluation logic, selection heuristics, risk models, platform logic, and non-public program data.

6.2 Duration. Confidentiality obligations survive termination:

Indefinitely for trade secrets; and

[5] years for other Confidential Information (or the maximum permitted by applicable law).

7. IP and Work Product (Platform Submissions)

7.1 If Tradequip Group requests Trader to produce platform-specific submissions (e.g., strategy writeups, research notes, annotated journals for training, code/indicators), Trader grants Tradequip Group a worldwide, perpetual, irrevocable license to use such submissions for evaluation and program operations. If assignment is permitted under applicable law, such work product is assigned to Platform Operator (or its designee).

7.2 Trader retains general trading skill and experience that does not disclose or rely on Tradequip Group Confidential Information.

8. Non-Circumvention of Tradequip Introductions

8.1 No circumvention. During the term and for 36 months after the last contact initiated by Tradequip Group, Trader shall not directly or indirectly solicit, contract with, accept capital from, or otherwise pursue a business relationship with a Tradequip Introduction, except through Tradequip Group or with Tradequip Group’s written consent.

8.2 No backchanneling. Trader shall not ask intermediaries to approach a Tradequip Introduction on Trader’s behalf.

8.3 Reasonableness. This Section is intended to protect Tradequip Group’s legitimate relationships and investments in building allocator/employer networks.

9. Opportunity Rights: Right of First Opportunity + Right of First Refusal (Employment and Capital Roles)

This Section is the core “vow” you described, triggered by selection performance.

9.1 Trigger: “Selected Candidate”

Upon written notice from Platform Operator or Management Company that Trader is a Selected Candidate, the following obligations apply.

9.2 Right of First Opportunity (exclusive first look window)

For 60 days after the Selection Notice Date (“Exclusive First Look Period”), Trader agrees:
a) Trader will engage in good-faith discussions with Management Company regarding employment and role placement; and
b) Trader will not accept any External Opportunity in the Restricted Industry during the Exclusive First Look Period without a written waiver from Management Company.

If Management Company does not extend an offer by the end of the Exclusive First Look Period, the Career Representation Period under Section 10 may still apply (unless waived).

9.3 Right of First Refusal (offer-matching)

If, during the Career Representation Period (Section 10), Trader receives a bona fide External Opportunity in the Restricted Industry, Trader must provide written notice with material terms. Management Company will have 15 business days to match the material economic terms and role scope. If Management Company matches, Trader agrees to accept Management Company’s offer, subject to lawful onboarding and regulatory requirements.

9.4 Right of First Offer (fundraising / starting a vehicle)

If Trader intends to raise capital, launch a trading vehicle, or accept third-party allocations in the Restricted Industry at any time during the Career Representation Period, Trader must first provide Management Company a 30-day exclusive negotiation right to sponsor the strategy within a Fund structure, employ Trader, or place Trader with an approved partner.

10. Career Representation and Placement Rights (Sports-agent style, but time-bounded)

10.1 Appointment as representative (6 months). For 6 months starting on the Selection Notice Date (“Career Representation Period”), Trader appoints Tradequip Group (through Platform Operator and/or Management Company) as Trader’s exclusive representative for negotiating and arranging any employment, engagement, PM seat, or capital allocation role in the Restricted Industry, including placement with Partner Management Companies.

10.2 Scope. During the Career Representation Period:
a) Trader will promptly disclose all inquiries/offers in the Restricted Industry to Tradequip Group;
b) Trader will not negotiate directly with prospective employers/allocators in the Restricted Industry except through Tradequip Group; and
c) Trader will not accept a Restricted Industry role arranged or introduced during the Career Representation Period without Tradequip Group’s written involvement, unless Tradequip Group fails to respond under Section 10.4.

10.3 Placement with Partner Management Companies. If Management Company does not hire Trader directly, Tradequip Group may place Trader with Partner Management Companies. Trader agrees to reasonably cooperate with interviews, compliance checks, and onboarding steps.

10.4 Response/waiver mechanism (prevents deadlock). If Trader receives a bona fide written offer in the Restricted Industry and Tradequip Group does not (i) engage within 10 business days after receiving full offer details, or (ii) provide a written plan to negotiate/close within a reasonable time, then Trader may proceed independently for that specific offer, provided Trader still complies with Sections 5 and 8 (data/track record and non-circumvention).

10.5 Fees.
a) Trader pays no placement fee. Trader will not be charged any placement fee by Tradequip Group.
b) Any placement or representation fee is payable solely by the hiring/engaging organization under a separate agreement with Tradequip Group, unless prohibited by law.

10.6 Later moves (switching management companies). If, after being employed/placed through Tradequip Group, Trader later seeks to change employers within the Restricted Industry, Trader agrees to provide Tradequip Group a first opportunity to represent and place Trader for a 6-month period starting from the date Trader provides written notice of intent to change employers (the “Re-Representation Period”), unless Trader is leaving the Restricted Industry entirely. Sections 10.4 and 10.5 apply similarly.

10.7 Compliance with local recruitment/agency laws. If applicable law requires licensing or imposes restrictions on exclusive representation, the Parties agree to modify this Section to achieve the closest lawful commercial equivalent (e.g., non-exclusive representation, disclosure-only, or partner-licensed representation), while preserving Tradequip Group’s non-circumvention rights.

11. Exclusivity During Active Evaluation (Optional Toggle)

[ON/OFF] During any period in which Trader is participating in a “funded trial,” “live evaluation,” or similar program defined in Schedule 2, Trader will not manage third-party capital or trade for another firm without written consent.

12. Termination

12.1 By Trader. Trader may terminate participation by closing the account (subject to identity/compliance retention and Sections 5, 6, 8, 9, 10 surviving as applicable).

12.2 By Tradequip Group. Tradequip Group may terminate for integrity violations, fraud, breach, or compliance concerns.

12.3 Survival. Sections 5 (data/track record), 6 (confidentiality), 8 (non-circumvention), 9–10 (opportunity/representation rights to the extent triggered), 14 (remedies), and 15–18 survive termination as applicable.

13. Independent Contractor; No Employment Relationship

Nothing in this Agreement creates an employment relationship. Trader is a platform participant unless and until a separate employment agreement is signed with Management Company.

14. Remedies

14.1 Injunctive relief. Trader acknowledges breach of confidentiality, data misuse, or non-circumvention may cause irreparable harm and injunctive relief may be appropriate where available.

14.2 Liquidated damages (optional, jurisdiction-sensitive).
[OPTIONAL] If Trader breaches Section 8 or 10 by circumventing a Tradequip Introduction or accepting a Restricted Industry role through backchannel negotiation during the Career Representation Period, Trader agrees to pay liquidated damages of USD [X] or [a reasonable estimate tied to actual lost placement fee], whichever is lower, to the extent permitted by law (not a penalty).

15. Notices

Notices shall be sent to the email addresses on file (and any additional addresses listed in Schedule 3).

16. Governing Law; Dispute Resolution

Governing law and forum/arbitration are specified in Schedule 4 (Country Addendum) based on Trader’s primary residence/work location.

17. Severability; Reformation

If any provision is unenforceable, it shall be modified to the minimum extent necessary to make it enforceable, and the remainder remains in effect.

18. Entire Agreement; Amendments

This is the entire agreement regarding Platform participation and Opportunity Rights. Amendments must be in writing (electronic acceptance permitted where lawful).

“Tradequip Introduction” means any investor, allocator, employer, recruiter, prop desk, fund, manager, or capital provider introduced by Tradequip Group to Trader or identified to Trader through non-public channels.
“Selection Notice Date” means the date Tradequip Group provides written notice that Trader is a Selected Candidate.
“Selected Candidate” means a Trader formally designated in writing as selected for professional placement consideration.

11. Post-Employment Career Representation (Limited; mirrors “teams trade players” concept)

If Employee seeks a new role within the Restricted Industry within 6 months after termination, Employee agrees to provide Company (and/or Platform Operator) a first opportunity to represent Employee for negotiation and placement for that role for 30 days after Employee provides written notice of intent to pursue such role.

11.2 If Company engages. If Company responds within 10 business days and elects to represent Employee, Employee will not backchannel around Company for 6 months in relation to employers/allocators contacted during the representation process, subject to Section 11.3.

11.3 No deadlock. If Company declines representation or does not materially engage within 10 business days, Employee may proceed independently for that role, subject to Sections 6, 8, and 10.4–10.5.

11.4 No employee-paid fees. Employee will not pay representation fees; any placement fee is paid by the hiring organization under separate agreement, unless prohibited by law.

11.5 Local law compliance. If local recruitment/agency laws restrict this Section, it shall be modified to the closest lawful commercial equivalent.

12. Return of Property; Records; Continuing Cooperation

12.1 Upon termination, Employee will return all Company property and information.

12.2 Employee will reasonably cooperate with audits, investigations, and regulatory inquiries related to the period of employment.

Thought for 3m 47s

Below is a copy/paste-ready Document 1 for embedding into your trader-selection platform signup flow, designed as a Global Master Agreement with country/region addenda that can be auto-loaded based on the trader’s selected country.

This template is drafted to (i) reflect your “opportunity creation” narrative, (ii) implement ROFO/ROFR + placement-agent rights, (iii) include a 21-day response window (with tolling + final notice mechanics to prevent “instant loophole jumps”), and (iv) provide a practical document-loading classification system.

You should still have counsel review the highest-risk jurisdictions (especially where “restraint of trade” doctrines are strict), because post-selection lockouts can be treated as non-compete equivalents and may need localized structure (e.g., converting to non-circumvention + confidentiality + paid garden leave, or limiting scope/duration). International non-compete enforceability varies substantially. 
GoGlobal
+2
GoGlobal
+2

A. Implementation approach for signup (recommended)
Option A (recommended): One Global Master + Addendum Pack (dynamic assembly)

Store:

GLOBAL MASTER TERMS (always shown), and

Addendum selected by the user’s country/region (e.g., ADD-IN, ADD-UAE, ADD-US, etc.).

Render on signup as:
“Global Master Terms + Applicable Addendum (Country/Region)”

Pros: scalable; consistent core; easy to patch globally.

Cons: requires careful “conflict/override” rules (included below).

Option B (simpler engineering, heavier legal ops): Full per-country documents

Pros: simpler runtime; every document is “as signed”.

Cons: harder to maintain at scale; duplicative edits.

Given your footprint, a hybrid works well: Top-priority countries get dedicated addenda; everyone else uses regional addenda.

DOCUMENT 1 — PLATFORM PARTICIPATION & OPPORTUNITY RIGHTS AGREEMENT
(GLOBAL MASTER TERMS + COUNTRY/REGION ADDENDUM FRAMEWORK)

Version: 1.0
Effective Date: The date you click “I Agree” (electronic acceptance)

This Platform Participation & Opportunity Rights Agreement (the “Agreement”) is between:

Tradequip (the platform operator), legal entity: [TRADEQUIP LEGAL NAME], organized in [JURISDICTION], with principal place of business at [ADDRESS] (“Tradequip,” “we,” “us”), and

You, the individual enrolling as a trader participant (“Trader,” “you”).

Tradequip operates a trader growth, discovery, and vetting platform (the “Platform”) that identifies high-performing traders through virtual performance, behavioral discipline, and rule compliance, and may offer selected traders opportunities to manage capital and/or become employed by or placed with aligned management companies.

1) PURPOSE, RECITALS, AND OPPORTUNITY STATEMENT

1.1 Opportunity Creation. The Platform is designed to help individuals discover and develop trading ability through structured evaluation, instrumentation, and performance measurement. You acknowledge that:
(a) Tradequip invests in building and operating the Platform, evaluation systems, and selection funnel;
(b) Your participation may create opportunities you might not otherwise access; and
(c) In exchange for access to the Platform and potential selection, you grant Tradequip the opportunity and placement rights in this Agreement.

1.2 No Guarantee. Participation does not guarantee selection, employment, capital allocation, compensation, or any particular outcome.

2) DEFINITIONS

2.1 “Affiliates” means entities controlling, controlled by, or under common control with Tradequip.

2.2 “Partner Management Company” means a third-party asset manager, fund manager, proprietary trading firm, or regulated entity that has a relationship with Tradequip for trader placement and/or capital management.

2.3 “Selected Trader” means a Trader designated by Tradequip (in writing or in-Platform notice) as selected for advanced tiers, interviews, placement, employment consideration, capital evaluation, or capital allocation processes.

2.4 “Opportunity” means any offer, proposal, invitation, term sheet, letter of intent, recruiter outreach, or arrangement (whether as employee, contractor, consultant, advisor, principal, partner, or otherwise) involving:

(a) trading for a firm or fund,

(b) portfolio management, capital allocation, or investment decision-making,

(c) raising or managing third-party capital, or

(d) forming, seeding, or operating a fund/managed account program,
in each case in the “Trading and Fund Management Industry” as defined below.

2.5 “Trading and Fund Management Industry” means trading, investing, market making, proprietary trading, asset management, hedge funds, managed accounts, copy-trading management, signal monetization, or any role involving discretionary execution or investment decision authority over capital.

2.6 “Platform-Sourced Opportunity” means an Opportunity that arises from (i) Tradequip, (ii) Tradequip Affiliates, (iii) Partner Management Companies, (iv) introductions made through the Platform, or (v) a counterparty who learned of you through Platform participation, leaderboards, marketing, referrals, or Tradequip/partner networks.

2.7 “Restricted Bypass Conduct” means doing any act intended to avoid Tradequip’s rights, including: routing through intermediaries, using alternate titles/entities, presenting an offer as “consulting” to disguise trading, signing via an affiliate, or delaying/withholding notice to run out a response window.

2.8 “Notice” means a written notice sent using the method in Section 9 and containing all information in Schedule E (Notice Form).

3) PLATFORM TERMS (HIGH-LEVEL)

3.1 Virtual/Evaluation Context. Unless and until separately documented, Platform trading is for evaluation and training. You are not depositing funds into a regulated brokerage arrangement through this Agreement.

3.2 Rules and Integrity. You agree to comply with Platform rules, risk limits, fair-use rules, and anti-manipulation policies. Tradequip may enforce rules, disqualify participants, or adjust tiers.

3.3 No Investment Advice. The Platform is not providing individualized investment advice.

4) CORE GRANT: RIGHT OF FIRST OPPORTUNITY + RIGHT OF FIRST REFUSAL + PLACEMENT AGENCY

This Section is the commercial core of the Agreement.

4.1 Right of First Opportunity (ROFO) — Employment / Capital / Placement

If you become a Selected Trader, then before you accept any Opportunity in the Trading and Fund Management Industry, you grant Tradequip the first opportunity to do one or more of the following (at Tradequip’s election):

(a) offer you employment with a Tradequip Affiliate or aligned management company;

(b) offer you a role managing capital under Tradequip-aligned structures;

(c) place you with a Partner Management Company; and/or

(d) negotiate a structured pathway (interviews, trials, team assignment, compensation terms).

4.2 Right of First Refusal (ROFR) — Matching Third-Party Opportunities

If you receive an Opportunity (including from third parties) and you are a Selected Trader, you must first present it to Tradequip via Notice. Tradequip has the right to match or improve the Opportunity (including through a Partner Management Company) subject to Section 5.

4.3 Appointment as Placement Agent (Exclusive During “Representation Window”)

If you become a Selected Trader, you appoint Tradequip as your exclusive representative/placement agent for Opportunities in the Trading and Fund Management Industry during the Representation Window:

Representation Window: begins on the date you are notified you are a Selected Trader, and lasts 6 months (the “Initial Window”).

Later Moves: If after being employed/placed through Tradequip or a Partner Management Company you later seek to change firms within the Trading and Fund Management Industry, you grant Tradequip an additional 6-month exclusive placement window commencing upon your written notice that you intend to move (the “Subsequent Window”), unless an Addendum limits or converts exclusivity to non-exclusive representation.

This “agency” concept is used because, in many jurisdictions, broad post-termination non-competes are restricted, and enforceability improves when structured as representation + non-circumvention + confidentiality rather than pure restraint. 
GoGlobal
+2
GoGlobal
+2

4.4 Platform-Sourced Opportunities — Perpetual Priority

For Platform-Sourced Opportunities, you grant Tradequip a perpetual right to be the presenting/placing party and to receive the benefits of this Agreement (including non-circumvention and remedies), because those Opportunities arise from Tradequip’s infrastructure and network investment.

If local law does not permit “perpetual” terms, the relevant Addendum automatically reduces this to the maximum enforceable period without invalidating the remainder (see Severability/Reformation).

5) NOTICE + 21-DAY RESPONSE MECHANISM (ANTI-LOOPHOLE DESIGN)
5.1 Mandatory Notice Before Any Acceptance or Negotiation

If you are a Selected Trader and you receive any Opportunity (whether solicited or unsolicited), you must:

(a) send Tradequip a complete Notice within 3 calendar days of receipt, and

(b) refrain from negotiating substantive terms (comp, start date, capital terms, carry, book, seeding, etc.) other than acknowledging receipt and stating: “Please coordinate through my representative, Tradequip.”

5.2 Tradequip Response Period — 21 Days (Calendar Days)

Upon Tradequip’s confirmed receipt of a complete Notice, Tradequip has 21 calendar days to issue one of the following elections in writing:

Match/Engage: Tradequip elects to match the Opportunity or engage you through Tradequip/Partner Management Company;

Waive: Tradequip waives in writing for that specific Opportunity;

Request Info: Tradequip requests missing information (tolls the clock);

Decline: Tradequip declines (Trader may proceed subject to Section 5.4).

5.3 Tolling to Prevent “Starve-the-clock” Loopholes

The 21-day response clock does not start until Notice is complete. If Tradequip reasonably requests missing information, the clock is paused until you provide it.

5.4 Final Notice + Cooling-Off (Prevents “Immediate Alternate Employment”)

If Tradequip does not respond within the 21-day period, you must send a Final Notice (email + in-app message) stating Tradequip is in non-response status. After Final Notice, you must wait an additional 7 calendar days before accepting the Opportunity.

This creates a minimum 28-day runway, eliminating “instant bypass” behavior while preserving fairness.

5.5 No Acceptance / No Start / No Side Letters During the Window

Until the earlier of (i) Tradequip waiver/decline, or (ii) expiry of the 21+7 day process, you must not:

accept the Opportunity,

sign any agreement (including “non-binding” LOIs if they contain exclusivity),

begin services, shadow-trade, access systems, or receive onboarding credentials,

route the Opportunity through an affiliate to disguise it.

Any such act is Restricted Bypass Conduct and a material breach.

6) POST-SELECTION “CAPITAL / FUNDRAISE LOCKOUT” (MODULAR, WITH LOCAL FALLBACKS)

Because enforceability varies globally, this clause is modular and may be modified by Addendum.

6.1 Trigger: “Protected Benefits”

This Section applies only if you receive any of the following from Tradequip/Affiliates/Partners (“Protected Benefits”):

(a) access to funded capital programs (real capital allocation, not pure simulation),

(b) stipends, guaranteed payments, training stipends, travel support,

(c) proprietary playbooks, systems, datasets, execution tooling, or confidential selection metrics,

(d) introductions to capital providers, allocators, or seed programs arranged by Tradequip.

6.2 Lockout Covenant (Default Global Form)

For [LOCKOUT_TERM_DEFAULT = 24 months] after the later of (i) receipt of Protected Benefits, or (ii) last day you had access to Protected Benefits, you agree you will not, without Tradequip’s written consent:

(a) solicit or raise third-party capital for a trading/fund product,

(b) form or operate a fund/managed account program, or

(c) manage discretionary third-party capital,
except through Tradequip or Tradequip-approved counterparties.

6.3 Mandatory Local Modifications

If the applicable Addendum (or local law) restricts or voids such lockouts (e.g., as a restraint of trade), then instead of a prohibition, the lockout is automatically converted to:

(i) a strict non-circumvention obligation (no Platform-Sourced counterparties),

(ii) reinforced confidentiality + trade secret protections, and

(iii) a reasonable compensation / liquidated damages structure tied to demonstrable platform-sourced value (where allowed), without invalidating the rest of the Agreement.

Examples of jurisdictions where post-termination restraint can be heavily limited include India (Section 27 doctrine in employment contexts), and parts of Latin America/Europe where compensation/proportionality is expected for enforceability.

7) NON-CIRCUMVENTION (GLOBAL CORE)

7.1 No Circumvention of Tradequip / Partners. You will not, directly or indirectly, circumvent Tradequip’s rights by:

approaching Partner Management Companies or Platform-introduced contacts outside Tradequip channels,

instructing third parties to present offers through alternate routes,

using a family member/entity to receive an offer that is intended for you.

7.2 Platform-Sourced Counterparties. If you engage any Platform-Sourced counterparty in the Trading and Fund Management Industry outside Tradequip channels within the applicable term, it is presumed circumvention unless you prove otherwise.

8) CONFIDENTIALITY, IP, AND PUBLICITY

8.1 Confidential Information includes Platform selection methods, risk rules, evaluation metrics, identity of partners/allocators, and any non-public technical or commercial information.

8.2 No Reverse Engineering / No Scraping. You will not scrape, reverse engineer, or attempt to derive proprietary selection logic.

8.3 Publicity. You may not use Tradequip’s name, partner names, or “Selected Trader” status in marketing without written consent, except truthful statements required by law.

9) NOTICE DELIVERY METHODS

Notices must be sent by:

Email: [LEGAL@TRADEQUIP.DOMAIN
] and [SECONDARY LEGAL EMAIL], and

In-App Notice: via the Platform notice/submit form (or in-app messaging) when available.

Receipt is the earliest of (i) confirmed email delivery, (ii) in-app timestamp, or (iii) written acknowledgement.

10) FEES AND DAMAGES (PLACEMENT / CIRCUMVENTION REMEDIES)
10.1 No Fee Charged to Trader (Default)

Tradequip does not charge you a fee to participate. Placement fees (if any) are generally paid by hiring/allocating counterparties.

10.2 Liquidated Damages for Circumvention (Jurisdiction-Dependent)

If you materially breach Sections 4–7 (Opportunity Rights / Notice / Non-Circumvention), you agree Tradequip may recover liquidated damages as a reasonable estimate of harm, as specified in Schedule F, plus costs of enforcement, unless prohibited by applicable law (in which case actual damages apply).

Because penalty rules vary, the Addendum may alter this to “actual damages + injunctive relief” or cap amounts to maintain enforceability.

11) INJUNCTIVE RELIEF / SPECIFIC PERFORMANCE

You agree that breach of Sections 4–8 may cause irreparable harm and that Tradequip may seek injunctive relief where available, in addition to damages.

(Some jurisdictions may limit injunctions for employment restraints; addenda address this.)

12.2 Lawful Bases (GDPR/UK GDPR where applicable). Where GDPR/UK GDPR applies, processing typically relies on contractual necessity, legitimate interests (platform integrity, anti-fraud, selection, security), and/or consent for optional marketing.

13) TERM AND TERMINATION

13.1 Term. This Agreement begins upon acceptance and continues while you maintain an account, and afterward to the extent necessary to enforce:

Platform-Sourced Opportunity rights (Section 4.4),

confidentiality/IP,

non-circumvention,

and any post-selection obligations triggered by Protected Benefits (Section 6).

13.2 Account Deletion Doesn’t Nullify Rights. Deleting your account does not terminate obligations for previously accrued rights and restrictions.

14) GOVERNING LAW / DISPUTE RESOLUTION (SET BY ADDENDUM)

14.1 The governing law and venue are determined by the Applicable Addendum for your country/region (Schedule A).

14.2 Mandatory consumer or labor protections in your jurisdiction may apply notwithstanding a chosen law clause (as applicable).

15) SEVERABILITY, REFORMATION, AND “MAXIMUM ENFORCEABLE” CONSTRUCTION

15.1 Severability. If any provision is held invalid, the remainder remains enforceable.

15.2 Reformation (“Blue Pencil” Where Permitted). Where a court may narrow an overbroad restriction, the parties request it be reduced to the minimum scope necessary to make it enforceable.

15.3 Maximum Enforceable Term. If “perpetual” or a stated term is not permitted, it automatically reduces to the maximum enforceable period in that jurisdiction without invalidating the rest.

16) ELECTRONIC ACCEPTANCE

By clicking “I Agree,” you consent to electronic contracting and acknowledge this Agreement is enforceable as a signed writing, subject to applicable law.

SCHEDULE E — NOTICE FORM (REQUIRED CONTENT)

A Notice is “complete” only if it contains:

Your full legal name + Platform username + email

Counterparty identity (legal name, address, website)

Role description (employee/contractor/PM/trader/etc.)

Comp structure (base/bonus/carry/split) or capital terms (AUM, draw, loss limits)

Start date / effective date / deadlines

Full documents received (offer letter, term sheet, recruiter emails)

How the counterparty found you (and whether Platform-Sourced)

Any exclusivity demanded by the counterparty

Your intended response timeline

SCHEDULE F — REMEDIES (DEFAULT; MAY BE MODIFIED BY ADDENDUM)

Default (Global): If you breach Sections 4–7 by circumvention or acceptance without completion of the Notice/ROFR process, Tradequip may seek (where lawful):

(a) injunctive relief,

(b) actual damages, and

(c) liquidated damages equal to the lesser of:
i) 12 months of your total cash compensation from the circumvented Opportunity, or
ii) a 20% placement-equivalent fee on first-year compensation (or, for capital programs, a 2% equivalent on allocated AUM for 12 months),
plus reasonable enforcement costs.

Where liquidated damages are treated as penalties, the Addendum converts this to actual damages plus lawful equitable remedies.

How to read: Each Addendum overrides the Global Master Terms only where it says so. All other sections remain unchanged.

ROFR/ROFO Characterization. Sections 4–7 are intended as commercial opportunity rights and non-circumvention, not an employment non-compete.

State Patchwork Acknowledgement. Non-compete-related enforcement is state-driven and varies materially; Tradequip will enforce restrictions only to the extent permissible in the applicable state.

DOCUMENT 1 — GLOBAL MASTER
TRADEQUIP PLATFORM PARTICIPATION + OPPORTUNITY RIGHTS AGREEMENT (GLOBAL MASTER)

Version: 1.0
Effective Date: [Auto-filled by system at acceptance]
Parties:

TradeQuip Platform Operator (“TradeQuip”, “we”, “us”, “our”), and its affiliates, including any management company employing traders and any fund/vehicle allocating capital (together, the “TradeQuip Group”); and

The individual accepting these terms (“you”, “Trader”, “Member”).

By clicking “I Accept”, you agree to this Agreement and any Country/Region Addendum that applies based on the country you select during signup.

1) PURPOSE AND THE OPPORTUNITY YOU RECEIVE

1.1 Purpose. TradeQuip operates a trader discovery, training, evaluation, and selection platform designed to identify high-potential trading talent, including individuals who may not have otherwise accessed professional trading or capital allocation opportunities.

1.2 Why these rights exist. You acknowledge that:
(a) the platform provides structured evaluation, ranking, feedback loops, and visibility that can lead to professional opportunities;
(b) TradeQuip invests in building and operating the platform, evaluation systems, coaching, compliance workflows, and partner networks; and
(c) in exchange for access to this opportunity pathway, you agree to the Opportunity Rights and Notice/Exclusivity obligations in this Agreement.

1.3 No guarantee. Platform access does not guarantee selection, employment, compensation, funding, or any particular outcome.

2) DEFINITIONS (CORE)

Key definitions appear here; additional definitions appear in Schedule A.

“Platform”: TradeQuip’s trader growth + vetting platform and related services.

“Opportunity”: any employment, engagement, capital allocation, funded trading seat, portfolio management role, or similar role in the Trading & Capital Management Industry that arises from, is connected to, or is influenced by your Platform participation, performance, ranking, visibility, referrals, introductions, or TradeQuip systems.

“Selected Group”: members formally designated by TradeQuip (written notice) as finalists/selected/shortlisted for hire, placement, or capital allocation.

“Partner Firms”: third parties with whom TradeQuip has (or is negotiating) a relationship for hiring, placement, or capital programs.

“Restricted Period”: the period defined in Section 6 (varies by status).

“Notice Period”: 21 calendar days from TradeQuip’s confirmed receipt of your Notice (Section 7).

3) ELIGIBILITY, ACCOUNT INTEGRITY, AND PERFORMANCE DATA

3.1 Truthful information. You will provide accurate identity, residency/country selection, and account information and keep it updated.

3.2 One person, one account (unless permitted). You will not create multiple accounts to manipulate rankings or eligibility unless TradeQuip authorizes in writing.

3.3 Evaluation and scoring. You agree TradeQuip may score, rank, and evaluate your trading behavior, risk behavior, and rule adherence using automated and manual review.

4) PLATFORM LICENSE + ACCEPTABLE USE

4.1 License. TradeQuip grants you a limited, revocable, non-transferable license to use the Platform for your personal participation.

4.2 Prohibited actions. You will not:
(a) reverse engineer or scrape the Platform;
(b) manipulate performance metrics;
(c) use bots to simulate trading activity;
(d) share access credentials;
(e) publish or disclose TradeQuip proprietary evaluation logic, rule sets, or detection methods.

5) CONFIDENTIALITY, TRADE SECRETS, AND PLATFORM IP

5.1 Confidential Information. Includes non-public: evaluation logic, selection criteria, scoring weights, risk rules, surveillance methods, partner lists, introductions, and any non-public reports.

5.2 Obligation. You will protect Confidential Information and not use it except for Platform participation.

5.3 Trade secret protection. This Section survives termination indefinitely to the maximum extent allowed by applicable law.

6) OPPORTUNITY RIGHTS (THE CORE ECONOMIC/CONTROL TERMS)

These provisions are designed to remain enforceable even where “non-compete” clauses are restricted by using (i) right of first opportunity/refusal, (ii) notice, (iii) non-circumvention, and (iv) liquidated damages/placement-equivalent fees.

6.1 Status Levels That Trigger Obligations

Your obligations depend on your status:

(A) General Member (default): You have confidentiality and anti-fraud obligations; no exclusivity yet.

(B) Opportunity Candidate: TradeQuip notifies you in writing that you are eligible for introductions, interviews, placement, or capital programs.

(C) Selected Group: TradeQuip notifies you in writing that you are shortlisted/selected for hire, placement, or capital allocation.

6.2 Right of First Opportunity (ROFO) — Opportunity Candidate

When you become an Opportunity Candidate, you grant TradeQuip a Right of First Opportunity for 6 months (the “Candidate Exclusivity Window”):

TradeQuip has the first right to present you to Partner Firms, negotiate roles, and propose TradeQuip Group employment/capital allocation.

During this 6-month window, you must route Industry Opportunities (defined in Schedule A) through TradeQuip first (Section 7 notice applies).

If a jurisdiction requires licensing for placement/agency, this clause operates only to the extent legally permitted, and TradeQuip may perform through licensed partners (Section 12.7).

6.3 Right of First Refusal (ROFR) — Selected Group (and Platform-Linked Opportunities)

When you enter the Selected Group, you grant TradeQuip a Right of First Refusal on:

any TradeQuip Group offer; and

any Platform-Linked Opportunity (any Opportunity connected to Platform participation as defined).

ROFR Mechanics:

You must provide a Notice of the third-party offer (Section 7).

TradeQuip has 21 calendar days to match or present a substantially equivalent offer (compensation or AUM terms, role scope, and reasonable commercial terms).

You may not accept, sign, commence work, commence managing capital, or fundraise under that offer until the Notice Period expires or TradeQuip responds in writing earlier.

6.4 Limited Post-Selection Industry Lockout (Time-Limited; Addendum Can Modify)

Default global position (subject to Addendum): For 12 months after the later of:
(i) your entry into the Selected Group, or
(ii) the end of any TradeQuip Group employment/capital program discussions that you initiated or received via the Platform,
you agree not to circumvent TradeQuip regarding Industry Opportunities, meaning:

you will not accept or commence an Industry Opportunity without providing Notice and honoring the ROFR/ROFO process; and

if you do accept such an opportunity without compliance, you will owe the fees in Section 9.

This is not drafted as a blanket “ban from working”; it is drafted as a notice + ROFR + anti-circumvention + fee framework to maximize enforceability.

6.5 Fund Formation / Fundraising / Capital Management Controls (Structured as ROFR + Anti-Circumvention)

If you are in the Selected Group (or if the contemplated activity is Platform-Linked), then for the Capital Control Period (default 3 years, Addendum may modify), you agree:

You will not fundraise, solicit capital, market a fund, form a fund/vehicle, or manage third-party capital in the Trading & Capital Management Industry in a manner that circumvents TradeQuip.

You must provide Notice and allow TradeQuip to:
(a) employ you via the TradeQuip Group; or
(b) place you with a Partner Firm; or
(c) endorse a firm/program in writing; or
(d) decline in writing.

If you proceed without complying, you owe the fees in Section 9.

7) NOTICE PROCESS (21 DAYS) + “NO IMMEDIATE SWITCH” SEAL
7.1 When Notice is required

You must give Notice if, during a Restricted Period, you:
(a) receive an Industry Opportunity;
(b) are invited to interview or negotiate for an Industry Opportunity;
(c) intend to start a fund/fundraise/manage third-party capital; or
(d) intend to join a firm in the Trading & Capital Management Industry.

7.2 Notice content

Notice must include:

the legal name of the firm/investor/counterparty;

role description;

compensation and/or AUM terms;

start date;

location/country; and

all written materials offered (redactions for unrelated personal info allowed).

7.3 The Notice Period and standstill

TradeQuip has 21 calendar days from confirmed receipt to respond. During that period you must not:

sign/accept the offer;

commence work;

commence trading or managing capital for that firm;

announce fundraising;

use an intermediary to “start now and paper later.”

Any attempt to start informally, through an affiliate, through a friend’s vehicle, through a “consulting” wrapper, or through an offshore entity you control is treated as circumvention.

7.4 TradeQuip response outcomes

TradeQuip may:

match the offer (employment or capital);

propose a substantially equivalent alternative;

approve the third-party opportunity; or

decline.

If TradeQuip declines in writing, you may proceed without fee only if you do so on the terms disclosed in your Notice.

8) NON-SOLICITATION + NON-INTERFERENCE (ENFORCEMENT-FRIENDLY CORE)

For 12 months after your last Platform-Linked Opportunity event (or longer if Addendum allows), you will not:

solicit TradeQuip employees/contractors to leave;

solicit Partner Firms introduced by TradeQuip to bypass TradeQuip;

interfere with TradeQuip’s relationships tied to your Platform pathway.

9) FEES, LIQUIDATED DAMAGES, AND PAYMENT STRUCTURE (UPDATED)

This Section is intended to fairly compensate TradeQuip for discovery/placement value and deter circumvention.

9.1 Placement-Equivalent Fee (Employment)

If you breach Sections 6–8 and accept/commence an Industry Opportunity (employment/engagement) without complying, you agree to pay TradeQuip a fee equal to:

40% of your first-year total cash compensation, defined as base salary + guaranteed bonus + sign-on bonus + guaranteed allowances tied to employment, earned or guaranteed within 12 months of start.

9.2 Capital Program Equivalent Fee (AUM)

If you breach Sections 6–8 and commence a capital allocation / funded trading / external AUM management arrangement without complying, you agree to pay:

4% of allocated AUM for 12 months, calculated on gross AUM allocated to you (or to a vehicle you manage/control) and payable monthly as 1/12 of 4%.

9.3 Who pays (Trader and/or Firm)

You agree TradeQuip may pursue payment from:
(a) you, and/or (b) the hiring firm/counterparty, if legally permitted, and/or (c) any entity you control that receives the economics.

9.4 Liquidated damages rationale

You agree actual damages are difficult to estimate (lost placement fees, opportunity costs, partner relationship impacts). Fees above are a reasonable estimate, not a penalty, and may be reduced only if a court requires.

9.5 Injunctive relief

Breach may cause irreparable harm. TradeQuip may seek injunctive relief in addition to monetary remedies to the extent permitted by law.

10) TERM, TERMINATION, AND SURVIVAL

10.1 Term. This Agreement begins on acceptance and continues while you have an account and thereafter only as needed to enforce surviving provisions.

10.2 Termination by TradeQuip. TradeQuip may suspend/terminate for fraud, manipulation, policy breaches, or legal compliance reasons.

10.3 Survival. Sections 5–9, 12, and Schedules survive as needed.

11) DATA PROTECTION AND PRIVACY

11.1 Data Processing Notice. TradeQuip processes personal data and performance data for platform delivery, security, compliance, evaluation, and selection. Details appear in Schedule B and are supplemented by your Addendum.

11.2 Cross-border transfers. Where required, TradeQuip will implement appropriate safeguards (contractual clauses, transfer tools, or equivalent) and/or local hosting where legally required.

12) GENERAL LEGAL TERMS

12.1 Addendum controls. If your Addendum conflicts with this Global Master, the Addendum controls only for that country/region.

12.2 Severability and reformation. If any provision is unenforceable, it is modified to the minimum extent necessary to be enforceable, and the rest remains effective.

12.3 Assignment. TradeQuip may assign to affiliates or successors. You may not assign without consent.

12.4 Electronic acceptance. Click acceptance constitutes a valid signature to the maximum extent permitted by law.

12.5 Language. English controls unless Addendum requires otherwise.

12.6 Dispute resolution. Governing law, venue, arbitration options, and mandatory consumer/employment constraints are addressed in the Addendum.

12.7 Placement/agency compliance. If local law regulates employment agency/placement activity, TradeQuip may (a) operate through licensed partners, (b) require additional consent forms, or (c) limit the “agency” aspects while maintaining confidentiality, anti-circumvention, and ROFR/Notice rights to the maximum extent permitted.

SCHEDULE A — EXPANDED DEFINITIONS (CORE)

“Trading & Capital Management Industry” / “Industry” includes: proprietary trading firms, hedge funds, asset managers, family offices, banks (markets desks), broker-dealers, CFDs/FX firms, crypto funds, or any role involving trading, portfolio management, execution, risk-taking authority, strategy research intended for trading P&L, or management of client/third-party capital.

“Industry Opportunity” includes employment, contractor roles, advisory with trading authority, signal-provision roles where compensation ties to trading performance, PM roles, funded seats, capital allocations, or “launch a pod/team” arrangements.

“Platform-Linked” means directly or indirectly arising from Platform participation (rankings, visibility, referrals, TradeQuip introductions, TradeQuip evaluation outputs, or Partner Firm discovery through the Platform).

SCHEDULE B — DATA PROCESSING NOTICE (GLOBAL BASELINE)

TradeQuip may process: identity/contact data, country/region selection, KYC where applicable, device/session security data, trading performance, behavioral analytics, rule violations, audit logs, and communications.

Purposes: account creation, security, fraud prevention, evaluation, ranking, selection, compliance, partner placement, dispute handling, and platform improvement.

Retention: minimum necessary for platform operation + compliance; extended retention for audit, dispute, and legal defense.

Sharing: within TradeQuip Group; with service providers (email/SMS, hosting, analytics); and with Partner Firms only when you are an Opportunity Candidate/Selected Group and only as needed for opportunities, subject to safeguards.

SCHEDULE C — STATUS TRIGGERS (OPERATIONAL)

Opportunity Candidate Notice: in-app + email notice stating your Candidate Exclusivity Window start date.

Selected Group Notice: in-app + email notice stating ROFR start date and Capital Control Period start date.

SCHEDULE D — FEES EXAMPLES (NON-BINDING)

Employment example: $200k first-year total cash → fee = 40% × 200k = $80k.

AUM example: $5,000,000 allocated AUM → fee = 4% × 5,000,000 = $200k paid over 12 months (~$16,666.67/month).

SCHEDULE E — ADDENDUM INCORPORATION

Your Addendum is selected by the country you choose at signup and incorporated by reference.

State/province-driven; noncompete restrictions vary; ROFR/notice emphasized. 
Federal Trade Commission

ADDENDUM-CA (CENTRAL AMERICA — GENERAL)

Local law; reasonableness; ROFR/notice emphasized.

ADDENDUM-SA (SOUTH AMERICA — GENERAL)

Local law; LGPD-style safeguards often relevant; reasonableness.

ADDENDUM-OCE (AUSTRALASIA / PACIFIC ISLANDS)

Local law; privacy and consumer rules; reasonableness.

ADDENDUM-IOI (INDIAN OCEAN ISLANDS)

Local law; privacy and consumer rules; reasonableness.

ADDENDUM-CEA (CENTRAL ASIA)

Local law; data localization may apply; feature limitation possible.
